We can implement this step with a min-heap, which has \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$O(\lambda _{\max }\log m)$$\end{document}O(λmaxlogm) worst-case update time.

Returning the cut edges. If the cut is from Step 2, we can return the edges of the cut with \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$O(\log m)$$\end{document}O(logm) time per edge by Lemma 23. The caveat is that these edges are only correct if we did not apply the connectivity sparsifier. This changes the factor \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\sqrt{\lambda _{\max }n}$$\end{document}λmaxn to \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\sqrt{m}$$\end{document}m in the update time. If the cut is from Step 3, we can return the edges with \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$O(\log m)$$\end{document}O(logm) time per edge by Lemma 16. \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\square $$\end{document}□

General \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\lambda $$\end{document}λ

We use the following result of Goranci, Henzinger, Nanongkai, Saranurak, Thorup, and Wulff-Nilsen [27].

Lemma 24

(Corollary 4.1 [27]) There exists a deterministic fully dynamic algorithm that, given a simple, unweighted, undirected graph \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$G = (V, E)$$\end{document}G=(V,E) with m edges and a parameter \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\phi \in (0, 1)$$\end{document}ϕ∈(0,1), maintains a min-cut estimate \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mu (G)$$\end{document}μ(G) in \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\tilde{O}(1/\phi ^3 +\phi m)$$\end{document}O~(1/ϕ3+ϕm) amortized time per edge insertion or deletion. If \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\phi \ge 240/\delta $$\end{document}ϕ≥240/δ, where \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\delta $$\end{document}δ is the minimum degree then the min-cut estimate is correct, i.e., \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mu (G) = \lambda (G)$$\end{document}μ(G)=λ(G).

It can return the edges of the cut in \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$O(\lambda \log m)$$\end{document}O(λlogm) time with the same update time.

In [27], they balance this with [8] to obtain an update time of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\tilde{O}(\tau ^{29/2}\sqrt{n}+m/\tau )=\tilde{O}(m^{29/31}n^{1/31})=\tilde{O}(m^{1-1/31})$$\end{document}O~(τ29/2n+m/τ)=O~(m29/31n1/31)=O~(m1-1/31) for \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\tau =m^{2/31}n^{-1/31}$$\end{document}τ=m2/31n-1/31. When we balance it with our Theorem 5, we obtain the following result. We note that since Theorem 24 only holds for simple graph, our combined result is also restricted to that case.

Theorem 6

There exists a deterministic dynamic algorithm that, given a simple, unweighted, undirected graph \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$G=(V,E)$$\end{document}G=(V,E), maintains the exact min-cut value \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\lambda $$\end{document}λ with amortized update time\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$ \tilde{O}(\min \{m^{1-1/12}, m^{11/13}n^{1/13},n^{3/2}\}). $$\end{document}O~(min{m1-1/12,m11/13n1/13,n3/2}).It can return the edges of the cut in \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$O(\lambda \log m)$$\end{document}O(λlogm) time with \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\tilde{O}(m^{1-1/12})$$\end{document}O~(m1-1/12) amortized update time.

Update time. We have amortized update time \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\tilde{O}(\tau ^{11/2}\sqrt{n}+\tau ^3+m/\tau )=O(\tau ^{11/2}\sqrt{n} +m/\tau )$$\end{document}O~(τ11/2n+τ3+m/τ)=O(τ11/2n+m/τ). To optimize for large values of m, we can use the connectivity sparsifier of Nagamochi and Ibaraki [55] again, which brings m down to \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\min \{m,\lambda n\}$$\end{document}min{m,λn}. Returning the cut edges. We take the version of Theorem 5 that can return the cut edges, which has \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\tilde{O}(\lambda _{\max }^5\sqrt{m})$$\end{document}O~(λmax5m) amortized update time, and do not apply the connectivity sparsifier. This gives an amortized update time of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\tilde{O}(m^{1-1/12})$$\end{document}O~(m1-1/12). \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\square $$\end{document}□

Arboricity

In this section, we first show a structural result: a relation between the fractional arboricity and the ideal relative loads of a tree-packing, see Section 3.1. We then give a deterministic dynamic algorithm that is efficient for small values of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\alpha $$\end{document}α, see Section 3.2. For simple graphs, we can combine this with the state of the art for densest subgraph approximation, see Section 3.3. For multi-graphs, we need to downsample the high \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\alpha $$\end{document}α regime to low \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\alpha $$\end{document}α regime. While this is relatively straight-forward against an oblivious adversary (Section 3.4), it is much more involved against an adaptive adversary (Section 3.5).

Structural Result

The idea is to show that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$(\min \ell ^{*}(e))^{-1} = \alpha (G)$$\end{document}(minℓ∗(e))-1=α(G). Then we can estimate \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\alpha (G)$$\end{document}α(G) by simply taking \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\alpha _{\mathrm{{est}}} = (\min \ell (e))^{-1}$$\end{document}αest=(minℓ(e))-1, with \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\ell $$\end{document}ℓ some good approximation of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\ell ^*$$\end{document}ℓ∗. For the integral arboricity, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\lceil \alpha \rceil $$\end{document}⌈α⌉, a similar result follows already from [1–3]. We achieve this, more nuanced result by using the language of ideal load decompositions from [8]. In particular, we use the following observation. We provide a proof for completeness.

Observation 25

([8]) For each \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$S\in \mathcal {P}^*$$\end{document}S∈P∗, we have \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Phi _{G[S]} \ge \Phi $$\end{document}ΦG[S]≥Φ.

Proof

We will prove this by contradiction. Suppose \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal {P}$$\end{document}P is a partition of S such that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathrm {part\_val}(\mathcal {P}) < \Phi $$\end{document}part_val(P)<Φ. Let \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal {P}'= (\mathcal {P}^*\setminus \{S\})\cup \mathcal {P}$$\end{document}P′=(P∗\{S})∪P be a partition of V, then we see that\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$ \mathrm {part\_val}(\mathcal {P}') = \frac{|E(G/\mathcal {P}')|}{|\mathcal {P}'|-1} = \frac{|E(G/\mathcal {P}^*)|+|E(G[S]/\mathcal {P})|}{|\mathcal {P}^*|-1+|\mathcal {P}|-1} $$\end{document}part_val(P′)=|E(G/P′)||P′|-1=|E(G/P∗)|+|E(G[S]/P)||P∗|-1+|P|-1\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$ < \frac{\Phi (|\mathcal {P}^*|-1)+\Phi (|\mathcal {P}|-1)}{|\mathcal {P}^*|-1+|\mathcal {P}|-1}= \Phi , $$\end{document}<Φ(|P∗|-1)+Φ(|P|-1)|P∗|-1+|P|-1=Φ,using that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$ \frac{|E(G/\mathcal {P}^*)|}{|\mathcal {P}^*|-1}= \Phi $$\end{document}|E(G/P∗)||P∗|-1=Φ and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\frac{|E(G[S]/\mathcal {P})|}{|\mathcal {P}|-1}=\mathrm {part\_val}(\mathcal {P}) < \Phi $$\end{document}|E(G[S]/P)||P|-1=part_val(P)<Φ. \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\square $$\end{document}□

Now we can show the main result.

Theorem 1

Let \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$G=(V,E)$$\end{document}G=(V,E) be an undirected, unweighted (multi-)graph, then\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \alpha (G) = \frac{1}{\min _{e\in E} \ell ^{*}(e)}. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}α(G)=1mine∈Eℓ∗(e).

Proof

We start by showing \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\alpha (G) \ge (\min _{e\in E} \ell ^{*}(e))^{-1}$$\end{document}α(G)≥(mine∈Eℓ∗(e))-1. We note that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$(\min _{e\in E} \ell ^{*}(e))^{-1}= \max _i \Phi _i$$\end{document}(mine∈Eℓ∗(e))-1=maxiΦi, ranging over all partitions appearing in the ideal partitioning. Denote the graph where this is maximized by \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$G^*$$\end{document}G∗, and its vertex set by X. By Lemma 25, we have that it does not further partition, hence \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal {P}^*=X$$\end{document}P∗=X, so \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$G^*/\mathcal {P}^*=G^*$$\end{document}G∗/P∗=G∗ and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$G^*=G[X]$$\end{document}G∗=G[X]. Now we can conclude\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} (\min _{e\in E} \ell ^{*}(e))^{-1}&= \max _i \Phi _i = \frac{|E(G^*/\mathcal {P}^*)|}{|\mathcal {P}^*|-1} = \frac{|E(G[X])|}{|X|-1}\\&\le \max _{Y \subseteq V} \frac{|E(G[Y])|}{|Y|-1} = \alpha (G). \end{aligned}$$\end{document}(mine∈Eℓ∗(e))-1=maxiΦi=|E(G∗/P∗)||P∗|-1=|E(G[X])||X|-1≤maxY⊆V|E(G[Y])||Y|-1=α(G).Next, we show that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\alpha (G) \le (\min _{e\in E} \ell ^{*}(e))^{-1}$$\end{document}α(G)≤(mine∈Eℓ∗(e))-1. Let \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$Y\subseteq V$$\end{document}Y⊆V be any subset. We will show that\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \frac{|E(Y)|}{|Y|-1} \le (\min _{e\in E} \ell ^{*}(e))^{-1}. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}|E(Y)||Y|-1≤(mine∈Eℓ∗(e))-1.Hence \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\alpha (G) = \max _{Y\subseteq V} \frac{E(Y)}{|Y|-1} \le (\min _{e\in E} \ell ^{*}(e))^{-1}.$$\end{document}α(G)=maxY⊆VE(Y)|Y|-1≤(mine∈Eℓ∗(e))-1.

The arguments we use here are very similar to the proof of Lemma 14, where we present them with more detail. We inspect the definition of ideal relative loads: let \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal {P}_0$$\end{document}P0 the partition for G with \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathrm {part\_val}(\mathcal {P}_0)=\Phi $$\end{document}part_val(P0)=Φ. Now we recurse on the subgraphs G[S] for each \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$S\in \mathcal {P}_0$$\end{document}S∈P0. We write the total decomposition as \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal {P}_i$$\end{document}Pi the partition on \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$G[S_i]$$\end{document}G[Si], where each \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$S_i\in \mathcal {P}_j$$\end{document}Si∈Pj for some \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$j< i$$\end{document}j<i, for \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$i\in \mathcal {I}$$\end{document}i∈I for some index set \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal {I}$$\end{document}I. Now we have the disjoint union:\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} E(G) = \bigcup _{i\in \mathcal {I}} E(G[S_i]/\mathcal {P}_i). \end{aligned}$$\end{document}E(G)=⋃i∈IE(G[Si]/Pi).So in particular we have\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} |E(Y)| = \sum _{i\in \mathcal {I}} |E(Y) \cap E(G[S_i]/\mathcal {P}_i)| \le \sum _{i\in \mathcal {I}} \Phi _i (|V_{Y,i}| -1), \end{aligned}$$\end{document}|E(Y)|=∑i∈I|E(Y)∩E(G[Si]/Pi)|≤∑i∈IΦi(|VY,i|-1),where \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Phi _i$$\end{document}Φi is the partition value of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal {P}_i$$\end{document}Pi, and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$V_{Y,i}=Y\cap V(G[S_i]/\mathcal {P}_i)$$\end{document}VY,i=Y∩V(G[Si]/Pi). We use here that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$|E(Y) \cap E(G[S_i]/\mathcal {P}_i)|\le \Phi _i (|V_{Y,i}| -1)$$\end{document}|E(Y)∩E(G[Si]/Pi)|≤Φi(|VY,i|-1), so let us prove that. Suppose \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$|E(Y) \cap E(G[S_i]/\mathcal {P}_i)|> \Phi _i (|V_{Y,i}| -1)$$\end{document}|E(Y)∩E(G[Si]/Pi)|>Φi(|VY,i|-1). Then we create the alternative partition \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal {P}_i'$$\end{document}Pi′ where we contract all edges of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$E(Y) \cap E(G[S_i]/\mathcal {P}_i)$$\end{document}E(Y)∩E(G[Si]/Pi). This gives partition value\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \Phi _i'&\le \frac{|E(G[S_i]/\mathcal {P}_i)|-|E(Y) \cap E(G[S_i]/\mathcal {P}_i)|}{|V_i|-1 -(|V_{Y,i}| -1)} < \frac{|E(G[S_i]/\mathcal {P}_i)|-\Phi _i (|V_{Y,i}| -1)|}{|V_i|-1 -(|V_{Y,i}| -1)}\\&= \frac{\Phi _i(|V_i|-1)-\Phi _i (|V_{Y,i}| -1)|}{|V_i|-1 -(|V_{Y,i}| -1)} = \Phi _i. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}Φi′≤|E(G[Si]/Pi)|-|E(Y)∩E(G[Si]/Pi)||Vi|-1-(|VY,i|-1)<|E(G[Si]/Pi)|-Φi(|VY,i|-1)||Vi|-1-(|VY,i|-1)=Φi(|Vi|-1)-Φi(|VY,i|-1)||Vi|-1-(|VY,i|-1)=Φi.But that contradicts the choice of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal {P}_i$$\end{document}Pi being the minimal partition.

Next, we prove that\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \sum _{i\in \mathcal {I}} (|V_{Y,i}| -1) = |Y|-1. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}∑i∈I(|VY,i|-1)=|Y|-1.We divide \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal {I}$$\end{document}I in different depths of recursion: let \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal {I}_j$$\end{document}Ij be such that for \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$i\in \mathcal {I}_j$$\end{document}i∈Ij we have \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$S_i\cap Y$$\end{document}Si∩Y in recursion depth j (i.e., it is in j partitions before)8. Let r be the total recursion depth. Then we can write\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \sum _{i\in \mathcal {I}} (|V_{Y,i})| -1)&= \sum _{j=0}^r \sum _{i\in \mathcal {I}_j} (|V_{Y,i}| -1) \\&= \sum _{j=0}^r \left( \sum _{i\in \mathcal {I}_j} |V_{Y,i}|\right) - |I_j|\\&= \sum _{j=1}^r |\mathcal {I}_{j}| -|\mathcal {I}_{j-1}| \\&= |\mathcal {I}_r| -|\mathcal {I}_{0}|\\&= |Y|-1. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}∑i∈I(|VY,i)|-1)=∑j=0r∑i∈Ij(|VY,i|-1)=∑j=0r∑i∈Ij|VY,i|-|Ij|=∑j=1r|Ij|-|Ij-1|=|Ir|-|I0|=|Y|-1.Now we use this as follows:\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \frac{|E(Y)|}{|Y|-1}&\le \frac{\sum _{i\in \mathcal {I}} \Phi _i (|V_{Y,i}| -1)}{|Y|-1} \\&\le \left( \max _{i\in \mathcal {I}}\Phi _i\right) \frac{\sum _{i\in \mathcal {I}}|V_{Y,i}| -1}{|Y|-1} \\&= \max _{i\in \mathcal {I}}\Phi _i\\&= (\min _{e\in E} \ell ^{*}(e))^{-1}, \end{aligned}$$\end{document}|E(Y)||Y|-1≤∑i∈IΦi(|VY,i|-1)|Y|-1≤maxi∈IΦi∑i∈I|VY,i|-1|Y|-1=maxi∈IΦi=(mine∈Eℓ∗(e))-1,where the last equality holds by definition of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\ell ^*$$\end{document}ℓ∗.

\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\square $$\end{document}□

Next, we use the fact that a greedy tree-packing approximates an ideal packing well to give an approximation of the fractional arboricity.

Lemma 26

A greedy tree-packing \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal {T}$$\end{document}T with \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$|\mathcal {T}|\ge \tfrac{24\alpha ^2\log m }{\lambda \varepsilon ^2}$$\end{document}|T|≥24α2logmλε2 trees satisfies, for \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\varepsilon \in (0,1)$$\end{document}ε∈(0,1),\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \left| \frac{1}{\min _{e\in E}\ell ^\mathcal {T}(e)}- \alpha \right| \le \varepsilon \alpha . \end{aligned}$$\end{document}1mine∈EℓT(e)-α≤εα.

Proof

The tree-packing \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal {T}$$\end{document}T contains\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$ \frac{24\alpha ^2\log m }{\lambda \varepsilon ^2}\ge \frac{6(1+\varepsilon )^2\alpha ^2\log m }{\lambda \varepsilon ^2}=\frac{6\log m \lambda }{\left( \frac{\lambda }{\alpha (1+\varepsilon )}\varepsilon \right) ^2} $$\end{document}24α2logmλε2≥6(1+ε)2α2logmλε2=6logmλλα(1+ε)ε2trees. We note9 that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\tfrac{\lambda }{\alpha (1+\varepsilon )}\varepsilon \le \tfrac{2}{1+\varepsilon }\varepsilon<2 \varepsilon <2$$\end{document}λα(1+ε)ε≤21+εε<2ε<2, so we can apply Lemma 12 with \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\eta = \tfrac{\lambda }{\alpha (1+\varepsilon )}\varepsilon $$\end{document}η=λα(1+ε)ε to obtain\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$ \left| \min _{e\in E}\ell ^\mathcal {T}(e)-\min _{e\in E}\ell ^*(e)\right| \le \frac{\varepsilon }{\alpha (1+\varepsilon )}. $$\end{document}mine∈EℓT(e)-mine∈Eℓ∗(e)≤εα(1+ε).Now we see that\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \frac{1}{\min _{e\in E}\ell ^\mathcal {T}(e)}- \alpha&\le \frac{1}{\min _{e\in E}\ell ^*(e)-\frac{\varepsilon }{\alpha (1+\varepsilon )}}- \alpha \\ &= \frac{1}{\frac{1}{\alpha }-\frac{\varepsilon }{\alpha (1+\varepsilon )}}- \alpha \\&= \left( \frac{1}{\frac{1+\varepsilon }{1+\varepsilon }-\frac{\varepsilon }{(1+\varepsilon )}}-1\right) \alpha \\&= \varepsilon \alpha . \end{aligned}$$\end{document}1mine∈EℓT(e)-α≤1mine∈Eℓ∗(e)-εα(1+ε)-α=11α-εα(1+ε)-α=11+ε1+ε-ε(1+ε)-1α=εα.The proof that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\alpha -\tfrac{1}{\min _{e\in E}\ell ^\mathcal {T}(e)}\le \varepsilon \alpha $$\end{document}α-1mine∈EℓT(e)≤εα is analogous. \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\square $$\end{document}□

Dynamic Algorithm for Bounded \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\alpha $$\end{document}α

In this section, we show how to dynamically maintain the estimate \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\tfrac{1}{\min _{e\in E}\ell ^\mathcal {T}(e)}$$\end{document}1mine∈EℓT(e), giving us the arboricity estimate. We start with a warm-up giving the result almost directly by plugging in a deterministic minimum spanning tree algorithm.

Warm-Up

Lemma 27

There exists a deterministic dynamic algorithm that, given an unweighted, undirected (multi-)graph \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$G=(V,E)$$\end{document}G=(V,E), maintains a \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$(1+\varepsilon )$$\end{document}(1+ε)-approximation of the fractional arboricity \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\alpha $$\end{document}α when \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\alpha \le \alpha _{\max }$$\end{document}α≤αmax in \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$O(\alpha _{\max }^4\log ^6 m / \varepsilon ^4)$$\end{document}O(αmax4log6m/ε4) amortized update time or a Las Vegas algorithm with \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$O(\alpha _{\max }^4 m^{o(1)} / \varepsilon ^4)$$\end{document}O(αmax4mo(1)/ε4) worst-case update time.

Proof

By Lemma 26, all we need to do is maintain \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Theta (\tfrac{\alpha _{\max }^2\log m }{\lambda \varepsilon ^2})=O(\tfrac{\alpha _{\max }^2\log m }{\varepsilon ^2})$$\end{document}Θ(αmax2logmλε2)=O(αmax2logmε2) greedy spanning trees, and then maintain the minimum over \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\ell ^\mathcal {T}(e)$$\end{document}ℓT(e). The latter can simply be done by maintaining a min-heap, which has an update time of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$O(\log m)$$\end{document}O(logm).

For the former, we note that any edge insertion or deletion can lead to overall \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$O\left( \left( \tfrac{\alpha _{\max }^2\log m }{\varepsilon ^2}\right) ^2 \right) $$\end{document}Oαmax2logmε22 updates to the spanning trees [6]. We can use a deterministic dynamic minimum spanning tree algorithm with\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$O(\log ^4 m)$$\end{document}O(log4m) amortized update time deterministically [21]; or\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$m^{o(1)}$$\end{document}mo(1) worst-case update time (Las Vegas algorithm) [23].Multiplying these update times with the number of spanning tree updates per insertion/deletion gives the result. Note that both subsume the \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$O(\log m)$$\end{document}O(logm) update time for maintaining the min-heap. \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\square $$\end{document}□

\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$O(\log ^4 m)$$\end{document}O(log4m) amortized update time deterministically [21]; or

\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$O(\log ^4 m)$$\end{document}O(log4m) amortized update time deterministically [21]; or

\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$m^{o(1)}$$\end{document}mo(1) worst-case update time (Las Vegas algorithm) [23].

\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$m^{o(1)}$$\end{document}mo(1) worst-case update time (Las Vegas algorithm) [23].

Recourse in Tree-Packing

Next, we show how we can bound the recourse in the tree-packing to shave a factor \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\alpha _{\max }$$\end{document}αmax. This is similar to Lemma 22, but has additional complications: to bound the recourse we need to guarantee a high min-cut, and we need to show we can keep an artificially high min-cut in a graph with certain arboricity. Note that there are even disconnected graphs with linear arboricity, so this is a non-trivial adaptation.

Theorem 7

There exists a deterministic dynamic algorithm that, given an unweighted, undirected (multi-)graph \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$G=(V,E)$$\end{document}G=(V,E), maintains a \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$(1+\varepsilon )$$\end{document}(1+ε)-approximation of the fractional arboricity \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\alpha $$\end{document}α when \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\alpha \le \alpha _{\max }$$\end{document}α≤αmax in \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$O(\alpha _{\max }\log ^6 m / \varepsilon ^4)$$\end{document}O(αmaxlog6m/ε4) amortized update time or a Las Vegas algorithm with \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$O(\alpha _{\max }^2 m^{o(1)} / \varepsilon ^4)$$\end{document}O(αmax2mo(1)/ε4) worst-case update time.

Proof

By Lemma 26, all we need to do is maintain \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Theta (\tfrac{\alpha ^2\log m }{\lambda \varepsilon ^2})$$\end{document}Θ(α2logmλε2) greedy spanning trees, and then maintain the minimum over \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\ell ^\mathcal {T}(e)$$\end{document}ℓT(e). As opposed to Lemma 27, we do not do this once with \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\alpha _{\max }$$\end{document}αmax, but keep \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\log \alpha _{\max }$$\end{document}logαmax copies corresponding to different values of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\alpha $$\end{document}α. The goal is to establish \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$O(|\mathcal {T}|^2/\alpha )$$\end{document}O(|T|2/α) recourse instead of the trivial \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$O(|\mathcal {T}|^2)$$\end{document}O(|T|2) recourse. We do this by utilizing that the recourse is \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$O(|\mathcal {T}|^2/\lambda )$$\end{document}O(|T|2/λ), and artificially increase the min-cut to \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Theta (\alpha )$$\end{document}Θ(α). This simultaneously means we only need to maintain \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$|\mathcal {T}|=\Theta (\tfrac{\alpha \log m }{\varepsilon ^2})$$\end{document}|T|=Θ(αlogmε2) greedy spanning trees.

To be precise, we maintain tree-packings \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal {T}_1, \mathcal {T}_2, \dots , \mathcal {T}_{\log \lambda _{\max }}$$\end{document}T1,T2,⋯,Tlogλmax, where \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$|\mathcal {T}_i|=O(2^{i} \log m/\varepsilon ^2)$$\end{document}|Ti|=O(2ilogm/ε2). We only need the packing \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal {T}_i$$\end{document}Ti when \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\alpha \in [2^i,2^{i+1})$$\end{document}α∈[2i,2i+1). If \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\alpha \notin [2^i,2^{i+1})$$\end{document}α∉[2i,2i+1), we still need the update times to hold, but we do not need the output to be correct. Each tree-packing \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal {T}_i$$\end{document}Ti is the tree-packing of some graph \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$G_i$$\end{document}Gi, where we show that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\alpha =\alpha (G_i)$$\end{document}α=α(Gi) if \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\alpha \in [2^i,2^{i+1})$$\end{document}α∈[2i,2i+1). As opposed to Lemma 22, we do not guarantee the stronger statement that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$G_i=G$$\end{document}Gi=G in this case.

First, we assume that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$G_i$$\end{document}Gi initially has min-cut \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\lambda \ge 2^{i-1}$$\end{document}λ≥2i-1. This means that initially \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\ell ^{\mathcal {T}_i}(e) \le \ell ^*(e)+\varepsilon /2^{i-1}\le 1/\Phi _{G_i}+1/2^{i-1}\le 2/2^{i-1}+1/2^{i-1}=O(1/2^i)$$\end{document}ℓTi(e)≤ℓ∗(e)+ε/2i-1≤1/ΦGi+1/2i-1≤2/2i-1+1/2i-1=O(1/2i), using Lemma 12 with \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\eta =1$$\end{document}η=1. Throughout the updates, we keep an edge e as a virtual edge if deleting e would cause any edge \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$e'$$\end{document}e′ to reach \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\ell ^{\mathcal {T}_i}(e') \ge 16/2^i$$\end{document}ℓTi(e′)≥16/2i. By the same arguments as in Lemma 22, this gives amortized recourse \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$O(|\mathcal {T}_i|^2/2^i)$$\end{document}O(|Ti|2/2i) for \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal {T}_i$$\end{document}Ti. We delete a virtual edge e if \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\ell ^{\mathcal {T}_i}(e)< 8/2^i$$\end{document}ℓTi(e)<8/2i after some update. This cannot lead to the load of any other edge rising above \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$16/2^i$$\end{document}16/2i, see Claim 1 in Lemma 22. Since any virtual edge was inserted as a real edge at some point, we can amortize the cost of this deletion. Next, we show that the virtual edges do not interfere with the arboricity if \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$2^i \le \alpha $$\end{document}2i≤α, in which case \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$8/2^i \ge 8/\alpha $$\end{document}8/2i≥8/α. We do this with two claims. If \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\ell ^{\mathcal {T}_i}(e)\ge 8/\alpha $$\end{document}ℓTi(e)≥8/α then \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\ell ^{*}(e) \ge 4/\alpha $$\end{document}ℓ∗(e)≥4/α.Proof. By Lemma 12 we have \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\ell ^*(e)\ge \ell ^{\mathcal {T}_i}(e)-\varepsilon /\alpha \ge 4/\alpha $$\end{document}ℓ∗(e)≥ℓTi(e)-ε/α≥4/α.Let \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\ell ^{*}(e) > \tfrac{1}{\alpha }$$\end{document}ℓ∗(e)>1α and let \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$S^*\subseteq V$$\end{document}S∗⊆V be such that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\alpha =\tfrac{|E(S^*)|}{|S^*|-1}$$\end{document}α=|E(S∗)||S∗|-1. Then \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$e \notin S^{*}$$\end{document}e∉S∗.Proof. Consider the partition induced by the last level of the ideal load decomposition \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal {P}^{*}$$\end{document}P∗, i.e., the classes of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal {P}^{*}$$\end{document}P∗ are the connected components induced by edges f with \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\ell ^{*}(f) = \tfrac{1}{\alpha }$$\end{document}ℓ∗(f)=1α. Note that by arguments similar to Lemma 14 and Theorem 1, we have 1) that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$e \in E(G/\mathcal {P}^{*})$$\end{document}e∈E(G/P∗) and 2) that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\frac{|E(G/\mathcal {P}^{*})|}{|\mathcal {P}^{*}|-1} < \alpha $$\end{document}|E(G/P∗)||P∗|-1<α. If \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$i = 1$$\end{document}i=1, we are done, so assume \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$i > 1$$\end{document}i>1. Observe first that the graph induced by \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$P_1, \dots , P_i$$\end{document}P1,⋯,Pi in \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$G/\mathcal {P}^{*}$$\end{document}G/P∗ contains strictly less than \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\alpha (i-1)$$\end{document}α(i-1) edges. Indeed, suppose for contradiction this is not so. If \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal {P}^{*}$$\end{document}P∗.Finally, we claim that for some \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} have \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$|E(G[S^{*}\cap P_{s}])| > \alpha (|S^{*}\cap P_{s}|-1)$$\end{document}|E(G[S∗∩Ps])|>α(|S∗∩Ps|-1) contradicting that G has arboricity \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\alpha $$\end{document}α. Indeed, observe that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \alpha = \frac{|E(S^*)|}{|S^*|-1}&\le \frac{|E(P_1, P_2, \dots , P_{i})|+\sum _{j = 1}^{i} |E(S^{*}\cap P_{j})|}{|S^*|-1} \\&= \frac{|E(P_1, P_2, \dots , P_{i})|+\sum _{j = 1}^{i} |E(S^{*}\cap P_{j})|}{(i-1)+ \sum _{j = 1}^{i} (|S^{*}\cap P_{j}|-1)}\\&< \frac{\alpha (i-1)+\sum _{j = 1}^{i} |E(S^{*}\cap P_{j})|}{(i-1) + \sum _{j = 1}^{i} (|S^{*}\cap P_{j}|-1)}, \end{aligned}$$\end{document}α=|E(S∗)||S∗|-1≤|E(P1,P2,⋯,Pi)|+∑j=1i|E(S∗∩Pj)||S∗|-1=|E(P1,P2,⋯,Pi)|+∑j=1i|E(S∗∩Pj)|(i-1)+∑j=1i(|S∗∩Pj|-1)<α(i-1)+∑j=1i|E(S∗∩Pj)|(i-1)+∑j=1i(|S∗∩Pj|-1), where we used that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$|S^{*}| = \sum _{j = 1}^{i} |S^{*}\cap P_{j}| = i + \sum _{j = 1}^{i} (|S^{*}\cap P_{j}|-1)$$\end{document}|S∗|=∑j=1i|S∗∩Pj|=i+∑j=1i(|S∗∩Pj|-1). This means that it suffices to approximate the arboricity in \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$G_i$$\end{document}Gi.

If \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\ell ^{\mathcal {T}_i}(e)\ge 8/\alpha $$\end{document}ℓTi(e)≥8/α then \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\ell ^{*}(e) \ge 4/\alpha $$\end{document}ℓ∗(e)≥4/α.Proof. By Lemma 12 we have \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\ell ^*(e)\ge \ell ^{\mathcal {T}_i}(e)-\varepsilon /\alpha \ge 4/\alpha $$\end{document}ℓ∗(e)≥ℓTi(e)-ε/α≥4/α.

If \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\ell ^{\mathcal {T}_i}(e)\ge 8/\alpha $$\end{document}ℓTi(e)≥8/α then \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\ell ^{*}(e) \ge 4/\alpha $$\end{document}ℓ∗(e)≥4/α.

Proof.
